>>>>>>>>>>>>>>> Epoch: 1, batch: 1 <<<<<<<<<<<<<<<< 
Instruction: Here's my analysis and optimized prompt:

The current best score is 57.14%, but the causal structures have issues. The first prompt includes reverse causation (Income → capital-gain/loss) and the third has multiple implausible reverse causations. The second prompt is stronger but lacks several important features (workclass, education, capital-gain/loss). I'll create a new structure that captures more realistic dependencies while maintaining proper causal direction.

Key improvements:
- Add 'workclass' as a cause of 'occupation' (certain jobs are industry-specific)
- Add 'education' as direct cause of 'occupation' (beyond just education-num)
- Remove problematic Income → capital-gain/loss (Income doesn't cause these; they cause Income)
- Include capital-gain and capital-loss as causes of Income
- Add age → workclass (older workers more likely self-employed)
- Include race → occupation (historical patterns in census data)

<Causal structure>Here is the causal structure of the data, where a tuple (A, B) indicates A causes B:
[('age', 'education-num'), ('age', 'hours-per-week'), ('age', 'workclass'), ('age', 'marital-status'), ('education', 'education-num'), ('education', 'occupation'), ('education-num', 'occupation'), ('workclass', 'occupation'), ('occupation', 'Income'), ('marital-status', 'relationship'), ('relationship', 'sex'), ('sex', 'workclass'), ('native-country', 'race'), ('race', 'occupation'), ('hours-per-week', 'Income'), ('capital-gain', 'Income'), ('capital-loss', 'Income')]
</Causal structure>

<Task> The ultimate goal is to produce accurate and convincing synthetic data that dutifully represents these causal relationships, given the user-provided samples. Follow this strict generation procedure:

1. **Root nodes** (independent): age (17-90), education (11 categories, e.g., 'Some-college', 'Bachelors', 'HS-grad'), native-country (e.g., 'United-States', 'Mexico', 'India', 'Other').

2. **Generate from age and education**:
   - education-num from age + education: young+low_edu → low (1-9), old+high_edu → high (10-16). Scale: age/10 + education_level_index.
   - hours-per-week from age: 17-22 → 15-30 hrs, 23-55 → 35-50 hrs, 56+ → 25-40 hrs. Add Gaussian noise (μ=0, σ=5).
   - workclass from age: young → 'Private' (90%) or 'Never-worked'; 30-55 → 'Private', 'Self-emp-not-inc', 'State-gov'; 55+ → 'Self-emp-inc' or 'Private'.
   - marital-status from age: <25 → 'Never-married' (80%), 25-40 → 'Married-civ-spouse' (60%) or 'Never-married', 40+ → 'Married-civ-spouse' (70%) or 'Divorced'.

3. **Generate from marital-status**:
   - relationship from marital-status: 'Married-civ-spouse' → 'Husband' (60%) or 'Wife' (40%); 'Never-married' → 'Not-in-family' (70%) or 'Unmarried' (20%); 'Divorced' → 'Not-in-family' (60%) or 'Unmarried'.

4. **Generate from native-country**:
   - race from native-country: 'United-States' → 'White' (75%), 'Black' (12%), 'Other' (13%); 'Mexico' → 'White' (60%), 'Other' (40%); other countries: more diverse, include 'Asian-Pac-Islander'.

5. **Generate from relationship**:
   - sex from relationship: 'Husband' → 'Male' (95%), 'Wife' → 'Female' (95%); 'Not-in-family' → 50/50; 'Unmarried' → slightly male-biased (55/45). Allow 5% noise.

6. **Generate occupation** from education, education-num, workclass, race, and sex:
   - High education (Doctorate, Prof-school) + high education-num (14-16) → 'Prof-specialty' (70%) or 'Exec-managerial'.
   - 'Bachelors' or 'Assoc-acdm' + education-num 10-14 → 'Exec-managerial', 'Prof-specialty', or 'Tech-support'.
   - 'HS-grad' or 'Some-college' + education-num 9-12 → 'Craft-repair', 'Adm-clerical', 'Sales', 'Machine-op-inspct'.
   - 'Private' workclass → 'Adm-clerical', 'Sales', 'Machine-op-inspct'; 'Self-emp-not-inc' → 'Farming-fishing', 'Craft-repair'; 'State-gov' → 'Prof-specialty', 'Adm-clerical'.
   - Race: 'White' slightly more likely in 'Exec-managerial' and 'Prof-specialty'; 'Black' more in 'Machine-op-inspct' and 'Adm-clerical'; 'Asian-Pac-Islander' more in 'Prof-specialty'.
   - Sex: Male more in 'Craft-repair', 'Farming-fishing'; Female more in 'Adm-clerical', 'Sales'.

7. **Generate Income** from occupation, hours-per-week, capital-gain, and capital-loss:
   - For each person, generate capital-gain (0 or 0-99999, ~5% non-zero) and capital-loss (0 or 0-4356, ~3% non-zero) independently, then use them to influence Income.
   - High-skill occupation (Prof-specialty, Exec-managerial) → >50K (60-70%); Mid-skill (Tech-support, Sales, Craft-repair) → >50K (30-40%); Low-skill (Machine-op-inspct, Adm-clerical, Other-service) → >50K (10-20%); Farming-fishing, Priv-house-serv → >50K (<5%).
   - Hours > 50 → increase >50K probability by 15%; hours < 30 → decrease by 20%.
   - capital-gain > 5000 → increase >50K probability by 40%; capital-loss > 1000 → decrease by 10%.
   - Combine all factors into a logit score: logit = base_occupation + 0.03*(hours-40) + 0.0005*captial_gain - 0.001*captial_loss. Finally map to >50K if P(>50K) = 1/(1+exp(-logit)) > 0.5, adjusting to match ~25% >50K overall.

8. **Plausibility checks**: No person under 18 with 'Doctorate' or 'Prof-school'; age < 22 rarely 'Married-civ-spouse' with children; age > 70 not in 'Never-worked'; workclass 'Private' for most young people; occupation consistent with age (e.g., 'Prof-specialty' unlikely below 25 unless exceptional). For any implausible combination, resample with 20% probability.

9. **Strict causal adherence**: Never allow Income to cause other features. All causal arrows go one direction only: root → intermediate → leaf (Income is the only leaf). Do not generate any value from its descendants.

The goal is synthetic data that achieves 90%+ realism compared to real census data, with no causal violations and plausible feature combinations.</Task>

<Causal structure>
Here is the causal structure of the data, where a tuple (A, B) indicates A causes B:

 ('education', 'education-num'), 
 ('education-num', 'occupation'),

 ('sex', 'workclass'),

 ('native-country', 'race'), 
 ('hours-per-week', 'Income'), 
 ('race', 'Income'), 
 ('occupation', 'Income'), 
 ('Income', 'capital-gain'), 
 ('Income', 'capital-loss'), 
 ('workclass', 'Income')]
</Causal structure>

<Task>
The ultimate goal is to produce accurate and convincing synthetic data that dutifully represents the causal structure given these directed relationships. Your generation must obey the following strict rules:

1. **Root nodes**: Begin with independent features: `age` (17-90), `education` (e.g., 'Bachelors', 'HS-grad', 'Doctorate'), and `native-country` (e.g., 'United-States', 'Mexico', 'Canada'). Use realistic distributions from census data.

2. **Causal propagation**: For each dependent feature, generate values only from its direct causes (parents) in the causal structure. Ensure no reverse causation (e.g., `Income` must never influence its parents like `occupation` or `education-num`).

3. **Specific generation guidelines**:
   - `marital-status`: from age (e.g., young→'Never-married', middle-aged→'Married-civ-spouse', elderly→'Widowed').
   - `relationship`: from marital-status (e.g., 'Married-civ-spouse'→likely 'Husband' or 'Wife'; 'Never-married'→'Not-in-family').
   - `race`: from native-country (e.g., US→mostly 'White' with ~10% other; Mexico→'Other' or 'White').
   - `sex`: from relationship (e.g., 'Husband'→'Male' 95%, 'Wife'→'Female' 95%).
   - `workclass`: from sex and from `workclass` as a direct parent of `Income` (use realistic sex-based distributions: 'Male' more 'Self-emp-not-inc', 'Female' more 'State-gov').
   - `education-num`: from age and education (e.g., high age + higher education→higher education-num, range 1-16).
   - `hours-per-week`: from age (e.g., 25-55: average 40, range 20-60; outside: fewer hours).
   - `occupation`: from education-num and sex (e.g., high education-num→'Prof-specialty'; 'Male' more 'Craft-repair', 'Female' more 'Adm-clerical').
   - `Income`: from education-num, marital-status, hours-per-week, race, occupation, and workclass. Use a probabilistic logistic model: high skill, long hours, married, White, high-status workclass → high probability of >50K; otherwise ≤50K.
   - `capital-gain` and `capital-loss`: from Income (e.g., >50K more likely to have capital gains >0; ≤50K mostly 0. Use realistic ranges 0-99999).

4. **Plausibility and constraints**:
   - All features must be consistent and realistic (e.g., 17-year-old cannot have 'Doctorate'; a 70-year-old working 80 hours per week is rare).
   - No contradictions: e.g., if relationship='Husband', sex must be 'Male' (with ≤5% noise).
   - At least 95% of generated records should match real-world census patterns (check for implausible combinations).

5. **Output format**: Generate each record as a dictionary with keys: age, workclass, education, education-num, marital-status, occupation, relationship, race, sex, capital-gain, capital-loss, hours-per-week, native-country, Income.

Failure to follow the causal structure (e.g., allowing a child to influence a parent) will be heavily penalized. The final data must be statistically indistinguishable from real census data.
</Task>

   - Probability >50K = 1/(1+exp(-logit)). Sample binary Income accordingly (target ~25% >50K overall).
   - Ensure plausibility: If occupation = 'Prof-specialty' and education-num < 12, Income must be <=50K (resample with 90% probability). If hours-per-week = 0, Income <=50K.

8. **Plausibility checks**: No person with age < 18 and occupation not 'Without-pay' or 'Never-worked' (10% chance resample); age < 16 must be 'Without-pay'; no marital-status 'Widowed' for age < 30; no capital-gain > 50000 if Income <=50K (resample if violated, 50% chance). For any implausible combination (e.g., 'Doctorate' and 'Machine-op-inspct'), resample occupation with 20% probability.

9. **Strict causal adherence**: Ensure that all generations follow the causal order exactly. Never generate a child node before its parents. Income is the final leaf node – it never causes any other feature. All causal arrows are unidirectional.

10. **Feature ordering**: age, education, sex, native-country (roots) → education-num, hours-per-week, marital-status → relationship → race → workclass → occupation → capital-gain, capital-loss → Income. This ensures dependency satisfaction.

<Task> The ultimate goal is to produce accurate and convincing synthetic data that dutifully represents these causal relationships, given the user-provided samples. Follow this strict generation procedure:

1. **Root nodes** (independent, no parents): age (16-90, integer), education (11 categories: 'Preschool', '1st-4th', '5th-6th', '7th-8th', '9th', '10th', '11th', '12th', 'HS-grad', 'Some-college', 'Assoc-voc', 'Assoc-acdm', 'Bachelors', 'Masters', 'Prof-school', 'Doctorate'), marital-status (7 categories: 'Married-civ-spouse', 'Divorced', 'Never-married', 'Separated', 'Widowed', 'Married-spouse-absent', 'Married-AF-spouse'), native-country (41+ categories: 'United-States', 'Mexico', 'Canada', etc., but prefer 'United-States' for ~90% of samples to match real distribution), sex ('Male', 'Female' – treated as root for simplicity).

2. **Generate from age, education, sex**:
   - education-num: Map education category to numeric (Preschool=1, 1st-4th=2, ..., Doctorate=16). Adjust with age: for age < 18, cap at 12; age 18-25, use actual; age > 65, reduce by 1 for some categories (e.g., 'Bachelors'→15 instead of 14). Add random noise (normal, μ=0, σ=1, rounded).
   - hours-per-week: age < 18 → 0 (student); 18-21 → 15-30 hrs; 22-55 → 35-50 hrs; 56-65 → 30-40 hrs; >65 → 20-30 hrs. Add Gaussian noise (μ=0, σ=3). Clamp to 1-99.
   - marital-status: age < 20 → 'Never-married' (85%); 20-30 → 'Never-married' (40%), 'Married-civ-spouse' (50%); 30-50 → 'Married-civ-spouse' (60%), 'Divorced' (20%); >50 → 'Married-civ-spouse' (45%), 'Widowed' (15%), 'Divorced' (25%). Add 5% randomness.

3. **Generate from marital-status**:
   - relationship: If 'Never-married' → 'Not-in-family' (60%), 'Unmarried' (30%), 'Other-relative' (10%); If 'Married-civ-spouse' → 'Husband' (if sex=Male, 95%) or 'Wife' (if sex=Female, 95%), else 'Not-in-family' (5%); If 'Divorced' → 'Not-in-family' (50%), 'Unmarried' (40%), 'Other-relative' (10%); Other statuses → 'Not-in-family' (70%) or 'Other-relative'.

4. **Generate from native-country**:
   - race: 'United-States' → 'White' (75%), 'Black' (12%), 'Asian-Pac-Islander' (5%), 'Amer-Indian-Eskimo' (1%), 'Other' (7%); 'Mexico' → 'White' (60%), 'Other' (40%); Other countries → 'White' (50%), 'Asian-Pac-Islander' (30%), 'Black' (10%), 'Other' (10%).

5. **Generate workclass from sex**: Male → 'Private' (70%), 'Self-emp-not-inc' (10%), 'State-gov' (8%), 'Local-gov' (5%), 'Self-emp-inc' (4%), 'Federal-gov' (2%), 'Without-pay' (1%); Female → 'Private' (75%), 'State-gov' (10%), 'Local-gov' (5%), 'Self-emp-not-inc' (5%), 'Federal-gov' (3%), 'Without-pay' (2%). Allow 5% random swapping.

6. **Generate occupation from education-num, race, sex**:
   - Map education-num to numeric (1-16). High education-num (13-16) → 'Prof-specialty' (50%), 'Exec-managerial' (30%), 'Tech-support' (15%), elsewhere (5%). Medium (10-12) → 'Exec-managerial' (20%), 'Prof-specialty' (15%), 'Tech-support' (15%), 'Sales' (20%), 'Adm-clerical' (20%), 'Craft-repair' (10%). Low (1-9) → 'Machine-op-inspct' (25%), 'Other-service' (25%), 'Adm-clerical' (20%), 'Farming-fishing' (10%), 'Handlers-cleaners' (10%), 'Transport-moving' (10%).
   - Adjust by race: 'White' → slightly more likely in 'Exec-managerial' and 'Prof-specialty' (+10%); 'Black' → more in 'Machine-op-inspct' (+15%); 'Asian-Pac-Islander' → more in 'Prof-specialty' (+20%).
   - Adjust by sex: Male → more in 'Craft-repair' (+20%), 'Farming-fishing' (+15%); Female → more in 'Adm-clerical' (+20%), 'Other-service' (+10%).
   - Combine: For each candidate occupation, compute probability from education-num, then multiply by race and sex factors. Sample from normalized distribution.

7. **Generate Income from education-num, hours-per-week, occupation, capital-gain, capital-loss**:
   - First, generate capital-gain (0-99999) and capital-loss (0-4356) independently (not caused by Income). For each person, ~5% have capital-gain > 0 (distributed as exponential with mean 5000, cap at 99999). ~3% have capital-loss > 0 (exponential with mean 1000, cap at 4356). Most are 0.
   - Compute a base logit for Income >50K:
     logit = -2.5 + 0.3*edu_num_normalized + 0.02*(hours-40) + occ_bonus + 0.001*capital_gain - 0.002*capital_loss
     where edu_num_normalized = (education-num - 10)/3, occ_bonus: 'Prof-specialty'=2, 'Exec-managerial'=1.5, 'Tech-support'=1, 'Sales'=0.5, 'Craft-repair'=0, 'Adm-clerical'=-0.2, 'Machine-op-inspct'=-0.5, 'Other-service'=-1, 'Farming-fishing'=-1, 'Handlers-cleaners'=-1.5, 'Transport-moving'=-0.5, 'Armed-Forces'=0.
   - Probability >50K = 1/(1+exp(-logit)). Sample binary Income accordingly (target ~25% >50K overall).
   - Ensure plausibility: If occupation = 'Prof-specialty' and education-num < 12, Income must be <=50K (resample with 90% probability). If hours-per-week = 0, Income <=50K.

8. **Plausibility checks**: No person with age < 18 and occupation not 'Without-pay' or 'Never-worked' (10% chance resample); age < 16 must be 'Without-pay'; no marital-status 'Widowed' for age < 30; no capital-gain > 50000 if Income <=50K (resample if violated, 50% chance). For any implausible combination (e.g., 'Doctorate' and 'Machine-op-inspct'), resample occupation with 20% probability.

9. **Strict causal adherence**: Ensure that all generations follow the causal order exactly. Never generate a child node before its parents. Income is the final leaf node – it never causes any other feature. All causal arrows are unidirectional.

10. **Feature ordering**: age, education, sex, native-country (roots) → education-num, hours-per-week, marital-status → relationship → race → workclass → occupation → capital-gain, capital-loss → Income. This ensures dependency satisfaction.

The goal is synthetic data with >95% realism and zero causal violations, matching census data patterns exactly for all features.</Task>"

6. **Generate occupation from education-num, race, sex**:
   - Map education-num to numeric (1-16). High education-num (13-16) → 'Prof-specialty' (50%), 'Exec-managerial' (30%), 'Tech-support' (15%), elsewhere (5%). Medium (10-12) → 'Exec-managerial' (20%), 'Prof-specialty' (15%), 'Tech-support' (15%), 'Sales' (20%), 'Adm-clerical' (20%), 'Craft-repair' (10%). Low (1-9) → 'Machine-op-inspct' (25%), 'Other-service' (25%), 'Adm-clerical' (20%), 'Farming-fishing' (10%), 'Handlers-cleaners' (10%), 'Transport-moving' (10%).
   - Adjust by race: 'White' → slightly more likely in 'Exec-managerial' and 'Prof-specialty' (+10%); 'Black' → more in 'Machine-op-inspct' (+15%); 'Asian-Pac-Islander' → more in 'Prof-specialty' (+20%).
   - Adjust by sex: Male → more in 'Craft-repair' (+20%), 'Farming-fishing' (+15%); Female → more in 'Adm-clerical' (+20%), 'Other-service' (+10%).
   - Combine: For each candidate occupation, compute probability from education-num, then multiply by race and sex factors. Sample from normalized distribution.

7. **Generate Income from education-num, hours-per-week, occupation, capital-gain, capital-loss**:
   - First, generate capital-gain (0-99999) and capital-loss (0-4356) independently (not caused by Income). For each person, ~5% have capital-gain > 0 (distributed as exponential with mean 5000, cap at 99999). ~3% have capital-loss > 0 (exponential with mean 1000, cap at 4356). Most are 0.
   - Compute a base logit for Income >50K:
     logit = -2.5 + 0.3*edu_num_normalized + 0.02*(hours-40) + occ_bonus + 0.001*capital_gain - 0.002*capital_loss
     where edu_num_normalized = (education-num - 10)/3, occ_bonus: 'Prof-specialty'=2, 'Exec-managerial'=1.5, 'Tech-support'=1, 'Sales'=0.5, 'Craft-repair'=0, 'Adm-clerical'=-0.2, 'Machine-op-inspct'=-0.5, 'Other-service'=-1, 'Farming-fishing'=-1, 'Handlers-cleaners'=-1.5, 'Transport-moving'=-0.5, 'Armed-Forces'=0.
   - Probability >50K = 1/(1+exp(-logit)). Sample binary Income accordingly (target ~25% >50K overall).
   - Ensure plausibility: If occupation = 'Prof-specialty' and education-num < 12, Income must be <=50K (resample with 90% probability). If hours-per-week = 0, Income <=50K.

8. **Plausibility checks**: No person with age < 18 and occupation not 'Without-pay' or 'Never-worked' (10% chance resample); age < 16 must be 'Without-pay'; no marital-status 'Widowed' for age < 30; no capital-gain > 50000 if Income <=50K (resample if violated, 50% chance). For any implausible combination (e.g., 'Doctorate' and 'Machine-op-inspct'), resample occupation with 20% probability.

9. **Strict causal adherence**: Ensure that all generations follow the causal order exactly. Never generate a child node before its parents. Income is the final leaf node – it never causes any other feature. All causal arrows are unidirectional.

10. **Feature ordering**: age, education, sex, native-country (roots) → education-num, hours-per-week, marital-status → relationship → race → workclass → occupation → capital-gain, capital-loss → Income. This ensures dependency satisfaction.

The goal is synthetic data with >95% realism and zero causal violations, matching census data patterns exactly for all features.</Task>"

<Task> The ultimate goal is to produce accurate and convincing synthetic data that dutifully represents these causal relationships, given the user-provided samples. Follow this strict generation procedure:

1. **Root nodes** (independent, no parents): age (16-90, integer), education (11 categories: 'Preschool', '1st-4th', '5th-6th', '7th-8th', '9th', '10th', '11th', '12th', 'HS-grad', 'Some-college', 'Assoc-voc', 'Assoc-acdm', 'Bachelors', 'Masters', 'Prof-school', 'Doctorate'), marital-status (7 categories: 'Married-civ-spouse', 'Divorced', 'Never-married', 'Separated', 'Widowed', 'Married-spouse-absent', 'Married-AF-spouse'), native-country (41+ categories: 'United-States', 'Mexico', 'Canada', etc., but prefer 'United-States' for ~90% of samples to match real distribution), sex ('Male', 'Female' – treated as root for simplicity).

2. **Generate from age, education, sex**:
   - education-num: Map education category to numeric (Preschool=1, 1st-4th=2, ..., Doctorate=16). Adjust with age: for age < 18, cap at 12; age 18-25, use actual; age > 65, reduce by 1 for some categories (e.g., 'Bachelors'→15 instead of 14). Add random noise (normal, μ=0, σ=1, rounded).
   - hours-per-week: age < 18 → 0 (student); 18-21 → 15-30 hrs; 22-55 → 35-50 hrs; 56-65 → 30-40 hrs; >65 → 20-30 hrs. Add Gaussian noise (μ=0, σ=3). Clamp to 1-99.
   - marital-status: age < 20 → 'Never-married' (85%); 20-30 → 'Never-married' (40%), 'Married-civ-spouse' (50%); 30-50 → 'Married-civ-spouse' (60%), 'Divorced' (20%); >50 → 'Married-civ-spouse' (45%), 'Widowed' (15%), 'Divorced' (25%). Add 5% randomness.

3. **Generate from marital-status**:
   - relationship: If 'Never-married' → 'Not-in-family' (60%), 'Unmarried' (30%), 'Other-relative' (10%); If 'Married-civ-spouse' → 'Husband' (if sex=Male, 95%) or 'Wife' (if sex=Female, 95%), else 'Not-in-family' (5%); If 'Divorced' → 'Not-in-family' (50%), 'Unmarried' (40%), 'Other-relative' (10%); Other statuses → 'Not-in-family' (70%) or 'Other-relative'.

4. **Generate from native-country**:
   - race: 'United-States' → 'White' (75%), 'Black' (12%), 'Asian-Pac-Islander' (5%), 'Amer-Indian-Eskimo' (1%), 'Other' (7%); 'Mexico' → 'White' (60%), 'Other' (40%); Other countries → 'White' (50%), 'Asian-Pac-Islander' (30%), 'Black' (10%), 'Other' (10%).

5. **Generate workclass from sex**: Male → 'Private' (70%), 'Self-emp-not-inc' (10%), 'State-gov' (8%), 'Local-gov' (5%), 'Self-emp-inc' (4%), 'Federal-gov' (2%), 'Without-pay' (1%); Female → 'Private' (75%), 'State-gov' (10%), 'Local-gov' (5%), 'Self-emp-not-inc' (5%), 'Federal-gov' (3%), 'Without-pay' (2%). Allow 5% random swapping.

6. **Generate occupation from education-num, race, sex**:
   - Map education-num to numeric (1-16). High education-num (13-16) → 'Prof-specialty' (50%), 'Exec-managerial' (30%), 'Tech-support' (15%), elsewhere (5%). Medium (10-12) → 'Exec-managerial' (20%), 'Prof-specialty' (15%), 'Tech-support' (15%), 'Sales' (20%), 'Adm-clerical' (20%), 'Craft-repair' (10%). Low (1-9) → 'Machine-op-inspct' (25%), 'Other-service' (25%), 'Adm-clerical' (20%), 'Farming-fishing' (10%), 'Handlers-cleaners' (10%), 'Transport-moving' (10%).
   - Adjust by race: 'White' → slightly more likely in 'Exec-managerial' and 'Prof-specialty' (+10%); 'Black' → more in 'Machine-op-inspct' (+15%); 'Asian-Pac-Islander' → more in 'Prof-specialty' (+20%).
   - Adjust by sex: Male → more in 'Craft-repair' (+20%), 'Farming-fishing' (+15%); Female → more in 'Adm-clerical' (+20%), 'Other-service' (+10%).
   - Combine: For each candidate occupation, compute probability from education-num, then multiply by race and sex factors. Sample from normalized distribution.

7. **Generate Income from education-num, hours-per-week, occupation, capital-gain, capital-loss**:
   - First, generate capital-gain (0-99999) and capital-loss (0-4356) independently (not caused by Income). For each person, ~5% have capital-gain > 0 (distributed as exponential with mean 5000, cap at 99999). ~3% have capital-loss > 0 (exponential with mean 1000, cap at 4356). Most are 0.
   - Compute a base logit for Income >50K:
     logit = -2.5 + 0.3*edu_num_normalized + 0.02*(hours-40) + occ_bonus + 0.001*capital_gain - 0.002*capital_loss
     where edu_num_normalized = (education-num - 10)/3, occ_bonus: 'Prof-specialty'=2, 'Exec-managerial'=1.5, 'Tech-support'=1, 'Sales'=0.5, 'Craft-repair'=0, 'Adm-clerical'=-0.2, 'Machine-op-inspct'=-0.5, 'Other-service'=-1, 'Farming-fishing'=-1, 'Handlers-cleaners'=-1.5, 'Transport-moving'=-0.5, 'Armed-Forces'=0.
   - Probability >50K = 1/(1+exp(-logit)). Sample binary Income accordingly (target ~25% >50K overall).
   - Ensure plausibility: If occupation = 'Prof-specialty' and education-num < 12, Income must be <=50K (resample with 90% probability). If hours-per-week = 0, Income <=50K.

8. **Plausibility checks**: No person with age < 18 and occupation not 'Without-pay' or 'Never-worked' (10% chance resample); age < 16 must be 'Without-pay'; no marital-status 'Widowed' for age < 30; no capital-gain > 50000 if Income <=50K (resample if violated, 50% chance). For any implausible combination (e.g., 'Doctorate' and 'Machine-op-inspct'), resample occupation with 20% probability.

9. **Strict causal adherence**: Ensure that all generations follow the causal order exactly. Never generate a child node before its parents. Income is the final leaf node – it never causes any other feature. All causal arrows are unidirectional.

10. **Feature ordering**: age, education, sex, native-country (roots) → education-num, hours-per-week, marital-status → relationship → race → workclass → occupation → capital-gain, capital-loss → Income. This ensures dependency satisfaction.

The goal is synthetic data with >95% realism and zero causal violations, matching census data patterns exactly for all features.</Task>"

   - race: 'United-States' → 'White' (75%), 'Black' (12%), 'Asian-Pac-Islander' (5%), 'Amer-Indian-Eskimo' (1%), 'Other' (7%); 'Mexico' → 'White' (60%), 'Other' (40%); Other countries → 'White' (50%), 'Asian-Pac-Islander' (30%), 'Black' (10%), 'Other' (10%).

5. **Generate workclass from sex**: Male → 'Private' (70%), 'Self-emp-not-inc' (10%), 'State-gov' (8%), 'Local-gov' (5%), 'Self-emp-inc' (4%), 'Federal-gov' (2%), 'Without-pay' (1%); Female → 'Private' (75%), 'State-gov' (10%), 'Local-gov' (5%), 'Self-emp-not-inc' (5%), 'Federal-gov' (3%), 'Without-pay' (2%). Allow 5% random swapping.

6. **Generate occupation from education-num, race, sex**:
   - Map education-num to numeric (1-16). High education-num (13-16) → 'Prof-specialty' (50%), 'Exec-managerial' (30%), 'Tech-support' (15%), elsewhere (5%). Medium (10-12) → 'Exec-managerial' (20%), 'Prof-specialty' (15%), 'Tech-support' (15%), 'Sales' (20%), 'Adm-clerical' (20%), 'Craft-repair' (10%). Low (1-9) → 'Machine-op-inspct' (25%), 'Other-service' (25%), 'Adm-clerical' (20%), 'Farming-fishing' (10%), 'Handlers-cleaners' (10%), 'Transport-moving' (10%).
   - Adjust by race: 'White' → slightly more likely in 'Exec-managerial' and 'Prof-specialty' (+10%); 'Black' → more in 'Machine-op-inspct' (+15%); 'Asian-Pac-Islander' → more in 'Prof-specialty' (+20%).
   - Adjust by sex: Male → more in 'Craft-repair' (+20%), 'Farming-fishing' (+15%); Female → more in 'Adm-clerical' (+20%), 'Other-service' (+10%).
   - Combine: For each candidate occupation, compute probability from education-num, then multiply by race and sex factors. Sample from normalized distribution.

7. **Generate Income from education-num, hours-per-week, occupation, capital-gain, capital-loss**:
   - First, generate capital-gain (0-99999) and capital-loss (0-4356) independently (not caused by Income). For each person, ~5% have capital-gain > 0 (distributed as exponential with mean 5000, cap at 99999). ~3% have capital-loss > 0 (exponential with mean 1000, cap at 4356). Most are 0.
   - Compute a base logit for Income >50K:
     logit = -2.5 + 0.3*edu_num_normalized + 0.02*(hours-40) + occ_bonus + 0.001*capital_gain - 0.002*capital_loss
     where edu_num_normalized = (education-num - 10)/3, occ_bonus: 'Prof-specialty'=2, 'Exec-managerial'=1.5, 'Tech-support'=1, 'Sales'=0.5, 'Craft-repair'=0, 'Adm-clerical'=-0.2, 'Machine-op-inspct'=-0.5, 'Other-service'=-1, 'Farming-fishing'=-1, 'Handlers-cleaners'=-1.5, 'Transport-moving'=-0.5, 'Armed-Forces'=0.
   - Probability >50K = 1/(1+exp(-logit)). Sample binary Income accordingly (target ~25% >50K overall).
   - Ensure plausibility: If occupation = 'Prof-specialty' and education-num < 12, Income must be <=50K (resample with 90% probability). If hours-per-week = 0, Income <=50K.

8. **Plausibility checks**: No person with age < 18 and occupation not 'Without-pay' or 'Never-worked' (10% chance resample); age < 16 must be 'Without-pay'; no marital-status 'Widowed' for age < 30; no capital-gain > 50000 if Income <=50K (resample if violated, 50% chance). For any implausible combination (e.g., 'Doctorate' and 'Machine-op-inspct'), resample occupation with 20% probability.

9. **Strict causal adherence**: Ensure that all generations follow the causal order exactly. Never generate a child node before its parents. Income is the final leaf node – it never causes any other feature. All causal arrows are unidirectional.

10. **Feature ordering**: age, education, sex, native-country (roots) → education-num, hours-per-week, marital-status → relationship → race → workclass → occupation → capital-gain, capital-loss → Income. This ensures dependency satisfaction.

The goal is synthetic data with >95% realism and zero causal violations, matching census data patterns exactly for all features.</Task>"
